Layer 4: Assessing the extent to which our assessment of the foundational components and financial forecasts is reflected or not in current company guidance, sell-side estimates and stock market narrative, primarily for the next 18 months and secondarily for 18 months to 3 years out

The Investment Analyst's heading The fourth set: Assessing the extent to which our assessment is reflected in current guidance, sell-side estimates and the stock market narrative

The fourth set as a whole C57 written last by the Investment Analyst; shown first here

Guidance, consensus and the narrative have caught up with the turnaround and not with its mechanics, which is the condition in which the gap between our assessment and the market's is favourable but not wide. Guidance is qualitative, moves only after evidence, is set to a consensus the company itself compiles and sits below delivery by construction; our operating-profit base is 3% to 5% above consensus for FY2026 and FY2027 and the probability of at least one further qualitative raise inside 18 months is about 60%, with September 2027 the most likely date. Consensus has upgraded FY2026 and will upgrade FY2027 operating profit at March and September 2027 with positive breadth, but its FY2027 EPS already assumes a tax rate the company has not guided, so the EPS line will stall or slip while the profit line rises, and a revision screen will read the first half of 2027 as a pause. The narrative is "turnaround delivered, now prove the cash": it has fully priced the cash doubt, the Power phasing, the disposal and DBAY, and it has not articulated the site-level operating leverage (the positive surprise), the tax rate embedded in consensus (the negative surprise) or the Components pull-forward (the FY2028 risk). The price at 186p sits at the consensus target and at the top of the stock's five-year multiple range with two Holds, so the favourable gap is in the earnings path rather than in the rating: the market will be surprised upward on EBIT, downward on EPS, and will be moved more by the March 2027 cash and dividend decisions and by DBAY than by either. For Richard, the case's standing under this set is that expectations are beatable on the line the company controls and already full on the lines it does not, that the sell-side has no numbers from the company and will keep following prints, and that the evidence on what investors beyond the four covering brokers believe is thin enough that the memo should say so.

Guidance

1 question
I-0162
I-01

How likely is company guidance to be raised, met or reduced at the next few earnings events (all events over the next 18 months), and what would drive each outcome? Secondarily, how likely is any guidance, mid-term target or ambition for 18 months to three years out to be raised, met or reduced?

C62

Question asked How likely is company guidance to be raised, met or reduced at the next few earnings events (all such events over the next 18 months), and what would drive each outcome? Secondarily, how likely is any guidance, mid-term target or ambition for 18 months to three years out to be raised, met or reduced?

Judgement: over the five events of the next 18 months the most likely sequence is hold, meet-and-beat, hold, raise, meet: a reiteration in November 2026, a FY2026 outturn above the £40m consensus in March 2027 with FY2027 framed "in line with" a consensus that will by then be £45m to £46m, a reiteration at the May 2027 AGM, a second qualitative raise at the September 2027 interims if the H1 2027 lap delivers, and a FY2027 outturn at or modestly above the framed number in March 2028. My probabilities: at least one further raise inside 18 months about 60%; guidance met without a raise about 25%; a reduction at any event about 15%, concentrated in the two windows in which TT has warned before (an unscheduled update in September to November, and the interim results). Guidance is favourable for the case not because it is low in absolute terms (it is qualitative and it moves only after a half is banked) but because the mechanism by which it moves (set to consensus, deliver, raise in words after evidence) means it will keep lagging a company that is, on our assessment, running 3% to 5% above consensus on operating profit for two years; the medium-term frame (12% undated, 4% to 6%, 90%-plus) is more likely to be dated and reiterated than raised, and the one internal number that functions as management's own plan (the LTIP range of 5% to 13% EPS growth a year from a 12.0p DTA-adjusted base) is at or below the street.

Event by event, with what drives each outcome. (1) November 2026 trading update (unannounced; issued in each of the last three Novembers). Hold about 65% ("trading in line with the upgraded expectations"; the honest-tracker posture does not raise twice in a half); raise about 25% (requires the H2 run rate to be visibly above £42m by October: Power's contracts shipping, Kuantan at rate, Components intake holding; management would more likely say "at the upper end" than give a number); cut about 10% (a Kuantan or Cleveland slip, a Power deferral into 2027, or a memory and PCB cost spike outrunning recovery; the 2024 pattern was a September update followed by a November one). A silent November would be mildly positive on the precedent. (2) FY2026 results, 10 March 2027. The outturn: above £40m about 70% (base £41.5m; the second half needs £23.0m against a reported £23.7m and clean £20.2m comparator), above £42m about 30%, below £40m about 15%. The framing of FY2027: "in line with market expectations" is the base (about 60%), as in March 2026, because this management sets the year to the compiled range and the range will have moved to £45m to £46m after the FY2026 beat; a framing that reads as a raise (building blocks that add to more than consensus, or the first dated margin target) about 25%; a cautious framing (macro, Components, tariffs) about 15%. The dividend: reinstated at a token level (1p to 2p) about 65%; deferred again about 35%, the trigger being a cash conversion print below 70% or a decision to hold cash for the Components perimeter. The tax statement in the annual report: "the plan does not yet support recognition" about 70%; partial recognition or a dated expectation about 30%. The comparator optics: reported H2 profit growth of about −3% on the headline (£23.0m against £23.7m) and +14% clean; the company has pre-explained this since March 2026 and Jefferies flagged it in November 2025, so the optical trap is known to the covering analysts and unknown to anyone reading the headline; it is a price risk, not a guidance risk. (3) AGM update, May 2027. Hold about 85% (four months of trading, organic growth positive for a second half, "in line"); a raise about 5% (this management does not raise on four months); a cut about 10% (the April 2025 cut came at this point in the calendar, with a chief executive departure; the trigger now would be a Power award pause under US budget delay or a Components reversal). (4) H1 2027 results, September 2027. This is where the second raise is most likely: raise about 40% (the H1 2027 lap of +6.5% delivers, the EMS margin is at or above 8.8%, cash conversion is back above 85% and the compiled range for FY2027 is still £45m to £46m against a run rate of £47m-plus); hold about 45%; cut about 15% (the second year of a recovery is where peers' misses came: working capital, transition friction, a restocking reversal in Components, an input-cost lag in the half). (5) FY2027 results, March 2028. Met or beaten about 65% against whatever the September framing was; the FY2028 framing again "in line". Unscheduled events that would reset guidance: a Components disposal (about 55% within 18 months; a sale would lower FY2027 and FY2028 revenue and profit guidance mechanically and raise the margin, so the headlines would read as a cut and the quality as a raise; the compiled consensus would need to be restated on the new perimeter, and the company's handling of that restatement is a communication risk); a DBAY move above 30% or a disposal of its stake; a renewed bid.

The medium term (18 months to three years). The 12% margin: reiterated undated about 50%; dated (for example "by 2029" or "by 2030") about 35%, most plausibly at a capital-markets event under the new Chair and chief financial officer in 2027, and most plausibly conditional on the disposal; withdrawn or lowered about 10%; raised below 5%. On today's perimeter the target needs £63m to £65m of profit and is not reachable inside three years on our base (10.0% in FY2029); with the disposal it comes into view for FY2029 or FY2030, so the probability of it being met inside three years is about 25% and depends on the disposal more than on operations. The 4% to 6% growth: met in FY2027 (5.0% on the lap) about 65%; at risk in FY2028 (4.0% with Components flat; a point of downside if the channel reverses) about 50%. The 90%-plus cash conversion: met in FY2028 about 55%; FY2027 at 85% to 90% should not be read as a miss. Leverage below 1.5x: met throughout, net cash by FY2028 on our base. The LTIP: the September 2026 change raised the EPS range to 5% to 13% a year over three years from a deferred-tax-adjusted base of 12.0p, so maximum vesting needs about 17.3p of DTA-adjusted EPS by FY2028 against our 17.9p (at a 29% rate, which is not fully DTA-adjusted) and consensus 17.8p; management's own full-stretch plan is therefore no higher than the street, which is the strongest single piece of evidence that guidance will go on being set below delivery. Peers for calibration: Volex guides floors and raises them (two upgrades in FY2027 already); discoverIE guides "ahead of expectations" and raised in October 2026; XP Power guided FY2027 up on 20 October 2026 on inflated orders; Solid State guides "not less than" and has been held back by award timing. TT's posture is closest to discoverIE's in form and least precise in content, and discoverIE's record (ten margin-target upgrades) is the path TT's new management is trying to copy from a lower base.

What would drive a reduction, in order of likelihood: a Kuantan ramp problem meeting a Power deferral in the same half (the correlated case, 15% to 20%); a Components reversal of the XP Power kind (orders inflated by lead times, then an air pocket), which would hurt FY2028 guidance more than FY2027; a memory and PCB cost spike in a half of fixed-price contracts; a sterling spike toward $1.40 (£4.5m of profit); a US continuing resolution extended through the first quarter of 2027. What would drive a raise beyond my base: the disposal at £30m or more with a larger dividend step; recognition of the US tax assets in the FY2026 accounts (which would not change operating guidance but would re-base the EPS the company talks about); Power at 17% on mix with the MBDA letter of intent converted to orders.

Sell-side estimates

1 question
I-0256
I-02

How likely are sell-side consensus forecasts, and the net breadth of revisions, to move higher or lower over the next few earnings events (over the next 18 months), and why? Secondarily, how likely are the consensus forecasts for 18 months to three years out to be upgraded, cut or held?

C56

Question asked How likely are sell-side consensus forecasts, and the net breadth of revisions, to move higher or lower over the next few earnings events (over the next 18 months): to be upgraded, cut or held, and why? Secondarily, how likely are the consensus forecasts for 18 months to three years out to be upgraded, cut or held?

Judgement: consensus operating profit is more likely to be upgraded than cut at each of the next three events (FY2026 EBIT from £40.0m toward £41.5m to £42m by March 2027; FY2027 EBIT from £45.0m toward £46m to £47m across March and September 2027), with the breadth positive (three or four of the four brokers raising) because the company's qualitative raises force every model up at once; consensus EPS will not follow in step, because the FY2027 EPS of 15.9p already embeds a tax rate of about 26% to 27% that the company has not guided and that the March 2027 annual report is unlikely to support, so the FY2027 EPS line is more likely to be held or trimmed (by 3% to 8%) over the next six months as brokers raise operating profit and lift their tax rates at the same time, and more likely to be upgraded after September 2027 if the H1 2027 lap delivers. The last quarter's revision pattern is the tell: FY2027 EPS consensus rose 14% in three months while FY2027 EBITDA and sales rose 2%, so most of the EPS upgrade cycle to date has been brokers lowering tax and interest, not raising profit; the next leg has to come from profit, and it will, but the tax leg has to give some of itself back first. The net result over 18 months is an upgrade cycle on EBIT of 5% to 8% cumulatively, an EPS consensus that ends the period near where it is today for FY2027 (15.5p to 16.5p) and modestly higher for FY2028, and a revision breadth that is positive on profit and mixed on EPS. For the out-years, FY2028 EBIT (£50.0m against our £51.5m) is more likely held or nudged up than cut, FY2028 EPS (17.8p against our 17.9p) held, and the FY2029 figures (two brokers) are too thin to call consensus and will move with whichever broker updates next.

Where consensus sits, and why it moves the way it will. The four brokers (RBC Hold 190p, Jefferies Hold 175p, Investec Buy 205p, Stifel Buy 155p) span FY2027 revenue of £490m to £521m and FY2027 EPS of 15.3p to 17.0p; the median EPS (15.9p) is RBC's, the mean 15.6p. Jefferies, the longest-standing voice, is the low on revenue and EPS and explicitly models slower tax normalisation and £35.5m of FY2027 net debt; Investec is the high on everything and has the only target well above the price. With four contributors the median moves when the middle two move, and the middle two (RBC, Stifel) updated most recently after the H1 results; the compiled consensus the company publishes will reset in March. Against our base, consensus is 3% to 4% below on EBIT in each of FY2026 to FY2028 and 7% below on FY2026 EPS, 3% above on FY2027 EPS and level on FY2028 EPS. The FY2026 EPS gap is the share count and the tax rate (we use 182m shares and 36%; a broker using FactSet's 186.4m and 37% gets 11.9p from our profit); the FY2027 EPS gap is tax alone.

Event by event. (1) November 2026 update: no estimate changes beyond rounding unless the update says "upper end"; the revision counts stay positive on target prices (which rose 40% in three months and have further to go only if estimates rise) and flat on buy share (60%, unchanged for three months). (2) March 2027: FY2026 actual above £40m settles the FY2026 line; FY2027 EBIT moves to £46m to £47m if the company's building blocks (full £6m of savings, Kuantan at rate, Power recovered) are confirmed, with all four brokers raising (breadth 100%); FY2027 EPS is where the brokers who still carry 27% will have to decide whether to follow the company's FY2027 tax guidance (likely "low-to-mid 30s" if the pattern of FY2026 holds), and the two or three that do will cut the EPS line even as they raise profit, so the median EPS for FY2027 probably ends March within 2% of where it started while the operating line is up 3% to 4%. This divergence between EBIT breadth (positive) and EPS breadth (mixed) is the specific prediction the Consumer should watch for; it would be read by a screen as "estimates stalling" when the operating story is improving. The dividend's reinstatement would add a DPS line to consensus (currently nil for FY2026) and is a small positive for breadth. (3) May 2027: no change. (4) September 2027: the H1 2027 print is the first in which growth, margin and cash all have to appear together; on our base it beats the half-year run rate implied by a £45m to £46m consensus and produces the second round of EBIT upgrades (FY2027 to £47m-plus, FY2028 to £51m to £52m) and the first EPS upgrades that are profit-led; a disappointing cash number would cap the upgrades even on a profit beat. (5) March 2028: FY2028 and FY2029 become the focus; FY2028 EBIT consensus likely settles at £50m to £52m, in line with or just below our £51.5m; FY2028 EPS at 17.5p to 18.5p depending on how the tax rate has been guided by then.

The out-years and the structural reasons consensus will lag. The AlphaSense guidance report's finding from Jefferies' strategy work (2 October 2026) that UK small-cap industrials entering their first upgrade cycle after multi-year downgrades keep revision ratios above 1.0 for about twelve months supports a positive drift; TT's own history supports it too (in the 2021 to 2022 upturn estimates rose for six quarters). Three things cap the drift: the tax rate already in consensus EPS (upgrades to EBIT are partly pre-spent at the EPS line), the honest-tracker posture (brokers will not get numbers from the company, so they move after prints rather than before), and the price (at 186p against targets of 155p to 205p, two Holds and a Buy with a target below the price mean that the next rating moves are as likely to be downgrades on valuation as upgrades on estimates; Stifel's 155p target is the most exposed). The Components disposal would cut consensus revenue by about £77m and EBIT by £2m to £3m on announcement and would be presented by the brokers as quality-accretive; the mechanical cut would be the largest single revision event of the period and would read as negative on a revision screen, so the Consumer should pre-empt it in the memo. Tax recognition in the FY2026 or FY2027 accounts would raise statutory EPS sharply and adjusted EPS toward 16.5p for FY2027; because the out-year consensus already carries a mid-20s rate, the recognition would confirm rather than upgrade FY2028 EPS, which is a point the market has not noticed. For 18 months to three years out: FY2029 EBIT of £52.9m (two brokers) against our £54.5m and FY2029 EPS of 19.9p against our 19.9p: held, with upward drift if the disposal or recognition occurs; there is no FY2030 consensus. The peer read-across over the period runs through the restocking names (discoverIE, XP Power, Volex) and affects TT's revisions only at Components; a cohort disappointment in December 2026 would not move TT's FY2027 EBIT consensus by more than £1m but would move its multiple.

Net breadth, quantified as best the data allow. The dashboard's revision-momentum score is 12 (positive); EPS revisions +6% over one month, +14% over three, +13% over six, −13% over twelve; target-price revisions +11%, +40%, +37%; buy-rating share unchanged at 60%. Over the next 18 months I expect: EBIT revisions positive at March and September 2027 (breadth three or four of four each time); EPS revisions roughly neutral to March 2027, positive from September 2027; target prices up with estimates but at a slowing rate; buy share flat to down (a Hold-on-valuation downgrade is more likely than a new Buy at 186p); and the twelve-month EPS revision turning positive from November 2026 as the March 2026 trough drops out of the window, which will improve the dashboard's sell-side momentum letter from C mechanically in the first quarter of 2027.

Stock market narrative

1 question
I-0355
I-03

How comprehensively are these dynamics articulated in the current narrative of (i) sell-side commentary and (ii) investor commentary: how well known is each of them to investors who own or cover the company or its sector, primarily for the next 18 months and secondarily for 18 months to three years out?

C55

Question asked How comprehensively are these dynamics articulated in the current narrative of (i) sell-side commentary and (ii) investor commentary (for example on Twitter): how well known is each of them to investors who own or cover the company or its sector, primarily for the next 18 months and secondarily for 18 months to three years out?

Judgement: the narrative has moved from "another warning is coming" to "turnaround delivered, now prove the cash" and has stopped there; of the twelve dynamics that decide the next two results, five are fully articulated (Power's margin slip as phasing, working capital and the cash-conversion test, the Components disposal as quality-accretive, the defence design-in thesis, DBAY as arbiter), four are articulated by one house and not by the market (the H2 comparator, the cost programme's size, the dividend timetable, the LTIP change), and three are not articulated at all in any document the AlphaSense report could find (the operating-leverage mechanism at the site level, the tax rate that consensus EPS already assumes against what the company guides, and the Components order pull-forward), and those three are the ones capable of surprising in either direction. The surprise skew is positive on operating profit (the leverage is under-modelled, so a clean FY2026 beat on the H2 EMS margin would be received as new information) and negative on EPS and on the Components line (the tax assumption in consensus and the channel pull-forward are both exposed to a plain restatement of facts the company has already disclosed). Two caveats govern the weight of this answer: the sell-side map is built almost entirely from Jefferies' own research (the AlphaSense report cites Jefferies in depth, RBC through a Sharecast summary, and neither Investec nor Stifel directly, so the coverage share is well below the 80% the request asked for), and the investor-commentary evidence (forums, social media) is assertion rather than citation, so I treat the "well known to investors" half of the question as low confidence and the "well known to the sell-side" half as medium.

Dynamic by dynamic, with who says what and since when (credit only where the house's own document is cited). (1) Operating leverage at Cleveland and Kuantan into a cut fixed base: Jefferies (2 September and 1 October 2026) describes the Cleveland turnaround as complete and the Suzhou transfer as de-risking, and RBC (3 September, via Sharecast) cites "self-help benefits", but no house models the drop-through per pound of returning volume, quantifies Cleveland's spare capacity or Kuantan's rate, or sets an EMS margin test; consensus's implied incremental margin (23%) is a residual, not a thesis. Unarticulated as a mechanism; the positive surprise lives here. (2) The £3m to £6m programme and the inflation that erodes it: the savings are in every note since February 2026; the erosion (wage inflation of 3.3% to 3.75% in the UK, Malaysia's minimum wage rising 17.6% from June 2027, compliance cost under Executive Order 14415, memory and PCB prices) is in no TT note and only in sector pieces; half-articulated. (3) Power's H1 2026 slip to 14.2%: fully articulated as phasing (Jefferies, RBC) and matched to the company's explanation; the counter-evidence (the former chief financial officer's March 2026 remark that Power margins would soften in 2026 on mix) is in a transcript and in no broker text. (4) The tax rate: every note states the mid-to-high-30s FY2026 rate and the 25.4% normalised figure, and Jefferies says explicitly that it models slower normalisation and that FY2026 EPS sits below consensus for that reason; but no note reconciles the FY2027 consensus EPS to the rate it implies, no note discusses the IAS 12 threshold or the mechanical first step (loss sheltering before recognition), and the possibility that part of the US loss pool sits in entities a Components sale would carry away is in no document. The EPS effect is treated as accounting rather than as a forecast line; the negative surprise lives here. (5) The H2 2025 comparator: articulated by Jefferies since 24 November 2025 (as £2.0m of non-repeat profit against the company's £3.5m of last-time-buy profit, a discrepancy the Consumer should note) and by the company itself; not articulated anywhere else; a headline reader in March 2027 will see H2 profit down 3%. (6) Working capital and cash conversion: the most comprehensively articulated dynamic; Jefferies calls cash "the key area requiring significant improvement" and models elevated net debt through FY2027; the market's scepticism here is already priced, which is why a print inside 70% to 80% is a positive surprise and a print below 70% is the confirmation of a known fear. (7) The Components disposal: articulated as quality-accretive and slightly dilutive by Jefferies (sum of the parts £24.9m blended, £11.5m on EBITA, £38.2m on sales) and as a catalyst by RBC; proceeds, buyers, stranded costs and the perimeter are unknown to everyone; the wide value range is itself the point. (8) The Components pull-forward (orders +26%, revenue +5.8%, XP Power's 20 October warning): the order growth is quoted approvingly in every note as momentum; no note stress-tests it; discoverIE's and XP Power's analysts have articulated the pull-forward risk for their own companies and nobody has applied it to TT. Unarticulated; the second negative surprise. (9) Defence design-in: fully articulated (Rolls-Royce, MBDA, Typhoon, the European book-to-bill of 135%, the UK Defence Investment Plan) and the most repeated element of the bull case; award lumpiness is acknowledged generically and not quantified. (10) DBAY: universally articulated, including the Takeover Code mechanics and the Cicor precedent; the sector's rule that cleaned-up mid-caps end in a sale is in Deutsche Bank's (discontinued) and Jefferies' sector pieces; the end-state thesis is well known. (11) The dividend: the March 2027 timetable and the "prudent level, build slowly" framing are in the notes; consensus carries nil for FY2026 and 2.5p for FY2027. (12) The LTIP: Jefferies' remuneration tracker (22 September 2026) recorded the range change to 5% to 13%; nobody uses it as a planning signal.

The business model and the peers, as the narrative frames them. The cohort narrative is restocking plus rearmament plus data centres: discoverIE (18.5x) and Volex (14.5x, +92% in twelve months) are the quality and the theme; XP Power is the live warning that orders can inflate; Solid State is award timing; Senior and Goodwin are the precedents that disposals and transactions re-rate UK engineered-component mid-caps; Kitron and Scanfil carry the European rearmament story with higher margins. TT is placed in that narrative as the cheap turnaround with a blockholder, which is accurate and which means its 10x is read as a discount to be earned rather than a bargain to be bought. For 18 months to three years out, the narrative knows the end-state (sale) and the undated items (12%, recognition) and does not model the path between: no note has a FY2029 margin above 10%, which is consistent with our base and inconsistent with management's target.

What is not known that could surprise, ranked by the size of the surprise and the probability it is sprung inside 18 months. (a) The FY2027 tax rate in consensus (negative, 5% to 10% of EPS, probability high that it is corrected by March 2027 when the company guides FY2027 tax). (b) The site-level drop-through (positive, 3% to 5% of FY2026 EBIT, probability about 70% that it shows in the H2 EMS margin). (c) The Components air pocket (negative, 1 to 1.5 points of group revenue and £2m of EBIT, probability about 40% inside 18 months, higher for FY2028). (d) The comparator optics (negative for a day, probability near certain that the headline reads as it does; the market's reaction depends on how the company leads its statement). (e) The US loss pool and the disposal perimeter (either direction, unknown size, probability of being raised publicly only if a sale is announced). On the investor-commentary side, the AlphaSense report's claim that forum and social-media commentary is simpler than the sell-side's (focused on the bid history, DBAY and the turnaround headline) is plausible and unevidenced; given the stock's liquidity ($1m a day) and the 29% blockholder, the marginal buyer is institutional and the sell-side narrative is the one that matters.

What this is: daily close-to-close returns over rolling windows of 126 trading days (about six months). Correlation: how closely the daily moves line up (1 = always together, 0 = unrelated). Appendix Research requests the Investment Analyst raised for the memo

Research requests for the next set

Company: TT Electronics plc (TTG-GB, London); a designer and manufacturer of engineered electronics for performance-critical applications in aerospace and defence, healthcare, and industrial automation and electrification, reporting from 2026 in three product-led divisions: Power (custom power conversion, magnetics and electromechanical assemblies, about two thirds aerospace and defence; FY2025 revenue £206.3m), EMS (high-mix low-volume electronic manufacturing services in the UK, North America, Mexico, Malaysia and China; £195.8m) and Components (passive and sensor components sold largely through distribution; £79.3m; under evaluation for divestment after a strategic review, per the interim results of 2 September 2026).

Request: OWN-1

Question: For TT Electronics plc (TTG-GB), which United States legal entities hold the £225.1m of gross unrecognised US tax losses disclosed in the 2025 annual report (within £282.4m of gross unrecognised losses, stated to have no expiry), what can be established from Companies House group accounts, US subsidiary filings, the 2024 and 2025 annual reports and broker research about how those losses are split between the entities that would form part of a Components divestment (the US Components businesses, including the Plano, Texas site closed at the end of 2025) and the entities that would remain (the Cleveland, Ohio EMS site, the Kansas City and Minneapolis Power sites), what Section 382 ownership-change limitations or other restrictions could apply given DBAY Advisors' holding of 29.04% (TR-1 of 5 August 2026) and the lapsed Cicor Technologies offer of January 2026, what TT Electronics' management and Deloitte have said about the five-year plan test for recognition (the 2025 annual report states the 2026 to 2030 plan does not yet support recognition and that timing depends on taxable profits at reassessment), and what precedents at UK-listed industrial companies that sold a loss-making US division show about whether unrecognised US losses were forfeited, transferred with the sale or retained by the continuing group, and how the adjusted effective tax rate moved in the two years before formal recognition?

Request: OWN-2

Question: For TT Electronics plc (TTG-GB), whose Components division reported H1 2026 order intake up 26% year on year (37% excluding the closed Plano site) against organic revenue growth of 5.8% and sells about 80% of its output through distribution (interim results and earnings call, 2 September 2026), what do the published results, inventory and lead-time disclosures of the electronic-component distributors (Avnet, Arrow Electronics, TTI, Digi-Key and Mouser where disclosed), the passive-component makers (Vishay, Bourns, Yageo, TE Connectivity), the sell-side distributor and semiconductor inventory surveys of September and October 2026, and expert interviews with distributor product managers and industrial OEM buyers show about current channel inventory in resistors, sensors, optoelectronic and passive components, the share of orders being placed early to secure supply as lead times extend (XP Power's 20 October 2026 update attributed its record third-quarter orders partly to customers ordering earlier than usual), the typical lag and depth of the subsequent air pocket when such pull-forward unwinds (with the 2022 to 2024 destocking at TT Electronics' distribution channel, which fell 27% organically in 2024, as the reference case), and what indicators, including TT Electronics' own Components book-to-bill, distributor days of inventory and lead-time series, would show the restocking pulse plateauing or reversing between November 2026 and the end of 2027?

Request: OWN-3

Question: For TT Electronics plc (TTG-GB), whose shares rose about 95% in the twelve months to October 2026 to a 24-month-forward price-to-earnings multiple of about 10.5x (FactSet, 16 October 2026) against a five-year median of about 7.2x and a ten-year median of about 9.8x, what do the share-price and forward-multiple histories of UK and European engineered-electronics and components companies show about how the multiple behaved in the 24 months after the first set of results that followed a profit-warning cycle and a change of chief executive, specifically at Volex plc (2017 to 2021), XP Power Ltd (2024 to 2026), Senior plc (2023 to 2025), discoverIE Group plc (2019 to 2021), Solid State plc (2024 to 2026) and TT Electronics itself (2016 to 2018 and 2020 to 2021), including the multiple at the first clean results, its peak and trough within the following 24 months, whether the re-rating was sustained once cash conversion recovered and dividends were reinstated, how the multiple responded to a disposal of a non-core division and to the presence of a blocking shareholder or a lapsed bid, and how sell-side target-price methodologies (Jefferies' 1 October 2026 note moved its target to 175p and applied a 5% discount to fair value; RBC's target is 190p, Investec's 205p, Stifel's 155p) and the covering brokers' published FY2027 building blocks (tax rate, Power and EMS margins, working capital, dividend) at Investec, Stifel and RBC compare with those of Jefferies, so that it is clear which broker's model drives the FactSet median and what multiple the sell-side regards as fair for a stock at this stage of recovery?
